IMPLEMENTER'S IMPLEMENTATION ON P02M0194 (2026-10-04 to 2026-10-06):

Scope: `docs/todo/P02M0194.md`, parts a to i - the classic foundations and pairing (a), LE beyond one mouse (b),
input (c), AudioService's device, voice and call model (d), A2DP and AVRCP (e), HFP and HSP over SCO (f), serial,
file push and tethering (g), LE Audio (h), and the operator tool, verification and completion (i). What stays open is
the owner's: the independent radio and peers (RootCanal and Bumble) and the codec oracles (AOSP SBC, liblc3). The
end-of-job runs are done: the three cross-builds, the regenerated dynamic report and its gate, and the Domain limits
read after the LE Audio gate's load.

## What was implemented

**The classic foundations (a).** `service_logic::{hci_bredr, l2cap_bredr, sdp, rfcomm, bt_pairing, bt_policy}`, each
with its suite: inquiry and paging, every BR/EDR pairing model through the operator's prompt watcher with levels
that are never lowered, the inbound policy per radio and per profile (PSM at L2CAP, server channel on RFCOMM), sixteen
L2CAP channels a link with enhanced retransmission (`Ertm`), an SDP client and server, and one RFCOMM session a link
with eight DLCs and credits - bounded writes (`room`, eight frames past the credits) and a paused reader (`pause`).
BluetoothService's `bluetooth_service/classic.rs` drives them. Gated: `bluetooth-classic`.

**LE (b).** Several links, privacy both ways (resolving lists, this host's own private address), every LE pairing
model, keys derived across transports both ways, the attribute server (`service_logic::gatt_server`) and applications'
GATT grants (`bluetooth-admin.mint-gatt`, PermissionManager's `bluetooth-gatt`). Gated: `bluetooth-le`.

**Input (c).** Classic HID and HID over GATT, gamepads into the gamepad set, a Bluetooth keyboard at the console
through InputService with its Ctrl+Alt+Delete and Power key acting on nothing. Gated: `bluetooth-input`.

**AudioService (d).** The `bluetooth-audio` root (one subscriber, never a role), outputs, voice devices and the
A2DP-sink route; `open-voice` and `voice-session` from `audio-admin.open-voices`; the call relay; per-device volume;
pacing (`audio_routing::{TimerPacer, OneForOne, Jitter}`). Gated: `audio-routing`, `bluetooth-audio`,
`bluetooth-le-audio`.

**Music (e) and calls (f).** `service_logic::{avdtp, avrcp, sbc, hfp}`: A2DP source and sink with SBC, AVRCP
absolute volume and the remote control, the HFP/HSP Audio Gateway with mSBC over SCO (H2 framing, one unit back per
unit in), the relayed call's indicators. Gated: `bluetooth-audio`.

**Serial, files and tethering (g).** `bluetooth-serial` grants per launch for one aliased peer (`btserial`);
`service_logic::obex` (CONNECT, PUTs, the final PUT, DISCONNECT; `PushServer`'s bound before a byte) over GOEP L2CAP
in enhanced retransmission or RFCOMM; `btctl send`/`receive` through the shell's redirections; `service_logic::bnep`
and NetworkService's `bluetooth-network` links with the uplink rule (`service_logic::uplink`). Gated:
`bluetooth-classic`, `bluetooth-transfer`.

**LE Audio (h).**
- THE ISO TRANSPORT: `drivers::bt_usb` cuts inbound bulk data by the four-byte header both kinds share and carries
  outbound `iso` on the bulk OUT pipe; BluetoothService classifies by handle (`service_logic::le_iso::classify`),
  reassembles SDUs, counts what no table names, and paces against the controller's ISO buffers (`LE Read Buffer Size
  v2`, Number Of Completed Packets on a stream's handle).
- THE CONTROLLER'S MODE: `LE Read Local Supported Features` read at initialisation - with extended advertising every
  scan and connection runs the extended commands (`le_scan_parameters`, `le_scan_enable`, `le_create_connection` on
  `Controller`), the legacy ones never mixed in; an extended report's whole data read for an RSI, its first 31 bytes
  as a legacy report's.
- LC3 (`service_logic::lc3`, written from the specification): every configuration, Appendix B's concealment, no
  heap; the suites reproduce Appendix C's four reference frames byte for byte and every intermediate step. The test
  data (`lc3/appendix_c.txt`) is extracted mechanically from the specification's text.
- UNICAST: `service_logic::{bap, ascs, cap, le_audio, gtbs}`; `bluetooth_service/le_audio.rs` - the walk (PACS,
  ASCS, CSIS with the SIRK decrypted under the bond's LTK, VCS), the coordinated set found by RSI and bonded as the
  set's (`pair_le`, not the operator's scan-bound `pair`), offered once to AudioService, its group set up and wound
  down in order (ASEs released, CISes disconnected, only then the CIG removed), music and calls taking it in turn.
- THE HOST'S GATT SERVER: GTBS (`host_server`, `le_server_writes`, `le_call`) and PACS.
- BROADCAST: `bluetooth_service/broadcast.rs`, `btctl broadcast scan|play|stop`, the operator's four calls.

**The operator, verification and completion (i).** `btctl` with every command the milestone lists; host suites for
every pure leaf; the gates registered in `check.sh`, the verification model's catalogue and its release-required set;
`docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` section 2.4.

**The in-guest fixture, written apart.** `drivers::{bt_world, bt_le_world, bt_le_audio, bt_peer, bt_sbc, bt_lc3}` and
`bt_fixture`: the controller and every peer the gates drive. Its LE Audio half (earbuds with PACS, ASCS, CSIS and VCS
servers and a GTBS client; a broadcast source with its BASE, BIGInfo and BIG; CIG, CIS, ISO and periodic sync on the
controller side) and its LC3 frame reader and writer were written by separate authors from the specifications alone,
without reading the host's LE Audio code or codec; the reader reproduces Appendix C's frames, and was also checked
against frames from liblc3 built outside the tree. The controller starts plain; `le-features 1` makes it an LE Audio
one from its next HCI Reset, so every older gate still runs the legacy LE paths.

## Defects found by the LE Audio gate and fixed

- The SIRK was decrypted with an all-zero key: `record()` returns a bond WITHOUT its keys (the store's list omits
  them); the walk now takes the LTK from `bond()` (the keyed lookup) and zeroes every copy.
- The set's other member was never heard: a controller reports each advertiser once per scan enable, and the member
  had been reported before the set's key was known. `scan_le` now restarts a running scan, and gives it its deadline
  back - or ends one it began - when the search is over, so an operator's next scan is not refused for it.
- The fixture died after a probe exited (a quiet exit, no panic): drivers now say why they leave their loop
  (`common::leaving`), which named it.
- A square-wave tone's third harmonic came out the loudest quantized line after LC3's tilted noise shaping; the probe
  plays sines.

## Departures and decisions

- The Generic Telephone Bearer notifies a call's state to, and takes Call Control Point writes from, only an encrypted
  peer trusted for voice (the milestone's wording); reads are answered to any connected LE peer.
- A coordinated set's further members are bonded with Just Works and trusted for audio without a prompt: the
  operator's pairing of the first member is the consent. A member that does not bond within ten seconds is let go.
- An LE Audio device is walked only on a controller with the CIS central role.
- AVRCP browsing waits with the media session the milestone excludes; enhanced retransmission is exercised by OBEX.

## Owner questions (asked at the end of the run)

- Adopting RootCanal and Bumble as the independent radio and peers; AOSP SBC and liblc3 as host oracles.
- Shipping SBC, mSBC and LC3 on a product that is not SIG-qualified; the SIG's member-only conformance material.
- Committing the LC3 Appendix C data extracted from the specification's text.
- The PAN access point: the `dnsmasq` harness peer waits on Bumble; the gate's access point is the fixture's own.

## Verification

Host suites: `cargo test --manifest-path user/services/logic/Cargo.toml` for the leaves above (LC3: 35 pass, 1 ignored
table print), `cargo test --manifest-path user/drivers/core/Cargo.toml bt_` for the fixture. Builds: all three
targets, and `./check.sh --gate dynamic-report` (92 tools on each). Guest gates on x86_64:
`bluetooth-classic`, `bluetooth-le`, `bluetooth-input`, `bluetooth-audio`, `bluetooth-transfer`, `bluetooth-service`,
`audio-routing` and `bluetooth-le-audio`.

Implementation update, 2026-10-09 (independent host codecs and USB bridge):

- Added `src/harness/prepare-bluetooth-oracles.py`, SHA-256/revision source pins and private Python version pins;
  dependencies stay under `.build/bluetooth-oracles`. AOSP SBC 745ee92b87ed62b5a8a1bac47d5df8cad623bf59, Google liblc3
  efe84d3e0ece9e54591098148da3d10f5503b84e, Google libsbc 6e505650145c9973d08a0bdd5e5f5e1914305e40, RootCanal 1.13.0 and
  Bumble 0.0.235. All codec sources are compiled unmodified; host adapters translate only ABI/framing. Source links,
  licenses, coverage and limitations are recorded in `src/harness/bluetooth-oracles.md`. No SIG member-only material
  was accessed and no product-qualification conclusion is made.
- Added and registered `bluetooth-codecs` in check.sh, verification catalogue and release-required set. It rebuilds
  the shipping `service_logic::{sbc,lc3}` leaves through `bluetooth-codec-host.rs`, independently crosses both
  encoders through both decoders, checks actual sample errors and retains source digests. The production guest does
  not link any oracle.
- The first independent LC3 quick run FAILED at 16 kHz/10 ms during 20-byte and 80-byte bitrate transitions: maximum
  normalized difference 0.03503418, RMS -59.58 dB, outside the published 0.00148/-89.06 dB precision limits.
  `lc3::ltpf::LtpfDecoder::run` now considers the effective activation bit AND nonzero bitrate-dependent gain.
  Previously an active zero-gain filter bypassed the next fade-in, and a zero gain with the active bit set bypassed
  fade-out. The four-line fix preserves the continuous histories and existing five transition cases.
- SBC investigation found reference defects, not a shipping-code defect. AOSP full-scale/low-bitpool four-band
  synthesis signed accumulator overflow was reproduced with `-fsanitize=signed-integer-overflow` at
  `synthesis-sbc.c:439` (retained `.build/bluetooth-oracles/aosp-overflow.log`). Precision vectors use a 12 dB margin
  and do not claim overload equivalence to this reference. AOSP four-band joint-stereo ReadScalefactors calls the
  aligned 4-bit reader with bitPtr=32 immediately after initialization, violating its <16 precondition; Google
  libsbc independently agrees with Liber on those exact AOSP bitstreams (maximum two PCM samples, -94.60 dB in the
  reproducer). Added Google libsbc as a complementary decoder for that configuration. AOSP remains the encoder
  oracle everywhere and the decoder oracle for every unaffected configuration. Google's frame-size API limit is
  explicitly respected; each case must have an independent decoder. Shipping SBC code is unchanged. Initial
  adapter setup failures (AOSP bitpool is set after initialization, and its HFP decoder also consumes one transport
  padding byte) were fixed in the host adapters, without changing guest codec framing.
- `BtBridge` in `usbredir_device.py` forwards real RootCanal H4 through existing Bluetooth USB control/interrupt/
  bulk/voice-alternate endpoints, tracks real successful CIS handles to classify outbound ISO, and fails on TCP
  loss or a bounded queue exceeding 1 MiB. It synthesizes no HCI command response, pairing or peer profile.

Implemented in bluetooth_classic_peers.py and bluetooth_guest_classic.py: Bumble AVDTP sink advertises delay reporting and sends150ms before stream opening; guest asserts the resulting160664us endpoint latency. ### GATT subscription repair and focused verification (2026-10-09T19:48:31Z)

Implemented the reviewed subscription defects in the production pure leaf `service_logic::att::subscription::Subscription`, registered under the existing ATT module and used by `bluetooth_service/gatt.rs`. The walk finds the exact requested characteristic value, derives notification setting 1 or indication setting 2 from its actual properties, discovers the next characteristic declaration to bound this value's descriptor range, and advances across both 16-bit and 128-bit descriptor pages. It validates expected ATT opcode, MTU, complete entry framing, ascending in-range handles, declaration/value relationships, matching error request and range, and the exact empty Write Response. The Bluetooth base UUID form of CCCD is recognized. Both property discovery and descriptor discovery are capped at the existing 32-entry ATT bound; no peer-sized table is retained. A CCCD from the next characteristic or outside the granted service is refused before any write.

The adjacent grant/failure review found that a dropped grant could still authorize a queued CCCD continuation, and a peer silent at a subscription step could hold the bearer forever. `next_gatt` now discards queued operations whose grant no longer exists. A subscription has one 30-second deadline covering queueing and every discovery/write step; no existing ATT timer constant existed to reuse. Queued expiry answers TimedOut without disturbing another operation's bearer. Outstanding expiry answers TimedOut, ends its queued application operations, quarantines that bearer and requests disconnection, preventing a late ATT reply from completing a newer operation. `bluetooth_service.rs` only adds the GATT deadline and timer hooks. Other application operation deadlines were not redesigned. A one-line adjacent fix makes the battery response's pdu[4] access lazy: an unexpected short response previously indexed it even when the length condition was false.

### Progress on the authorized independent microphone API (2026-10-09T21:02:20Z)

Implemented additive `audio-control.microphone-volume` / `set-microphone-volume` operations 6/7, Bluetooth-side microphone operations 5/6, and the appended `AudioEvent::MicrophoneVolume` tag 4. Existing device/endpoint records, event tags 0–3 and old operation numbers remain unchanged. `audioctl microphone DEVICE [LEVEL]` and the shared audio-client wrapper/provider expose the API. No sound-card driver command was invented: existing providers use AudioService's software capture scaling; their legacy combined level remains until the first explicit microphone setting, and merely reading the microphone level never changes that compatibility state. An explicit microphone setting then remains independent of subsequent speaker changes. Devices without recording input, including route endpoints, reject microphone control; values above 100 and unknown devices retain their typed failures.

HFP/HSP `Gateway` now retains both gains, including remote values received before its endpoint is offered. Remote `AT+VGM` updates the microphone alone, and operator control sends `+VGM`. The applied hardware value is read back after 0–15 quantization; hardware microphone samples are not scaled again. Initial offers and re-subscribed snapshots carry the current microphone value after Arrived. An unreported hardware Bluetooth microphone returns Unsupported, rather than pretending its speaker gain is the microphone gain. Accepted operator changes also enter the same FIFO event stream, so an older queued remote VGM cannot leave AudioService's final value stale.

Review found and corrected two material issues before declaring the source ready. The first draft synchronously queried every arriving input endpoint; an older stack silently ignores the new opcode, so this could block audio. Arrival now uses only the event stream and sends no new operation to an older stack. An explicit microphone setter has a two-second deadline, and any uncertain transport/framing response retires the Bluetooth control/event connection and its endpoint channels, preventing a late reply from completing a later request. Only an actual Unsupported response permits software fallback; other failures propagate. Second, the existing convenience RFCOMM sender discards enqueue failure. The new mic path instead uses `hfp::Gateway::queue_microphone_gain`, which checks an open DLC and the existing `Session::room` bound, queues the exact line, and only then commits the gain. Closed/full/invalid refusals preserve the previous state. Existing speaker semantics were left intact.

Runtime protocol-info still names `liber:bluetooth` major 1; the broker audio authority has no fingerprint negotiation. The host compatibility test freezes the pre-change event reader and proves that a new microphone message is skipped, then subsequent existing speaker/call/departure frames are read normally; unknown frames carry no capabilities and are consumed separately by the existing AudioService receive loop.

IMPLEMENTER'S INITIAL IMPLEMENTATION ON P02M0194 (2026-10-10 07:45:27 UTC):

The failed audio fixture exposed a concrete adjacent kernel defect in SYS_WAIT, SYS_WAIT_ANY and SYS_WAITSET_WAIT. Each first checks object readiness, then compares the earliest scheduler wake deadline (which can be a member Timer) against the current tick and returns ERR_TIMED_OUT. A Timer becoming ready between those steps can therefore incorrectly time out even caller deadline0. The actual fixture uses one locally owned timer with no concurrent rearm; scheduler register-then-recheck already handles this readiness transition. Independent read-only review agreed on all three paths.

Beginning the minimum correction in src/kernel/syscall/mod.rs: only a nonzero caller deadline may produce timeout; member timer deadlines still wake the scheduler. A deterministic actual-syscall regression in syscall/tests.rs will expose a real Timer through a test object and arm it exactly between readiness and deadline queries, without changing production clock or Timer APIs. Existing source edits and audit bytes are preserved. Implementation and verification are not yet complete; original audio failure, unperformed remaining gates and all image/source identities remain authoritative. No host module operations or broad reruns are authorized by this correction.

Applied the reviewed minimum production fix: exactly three timeout comparisons in sys_wait, sys_wait_any and sys_waitset_wait now consult the caller deadline, preserving timer-derived scheduler deadlines and register-then-recheck behavior. The three new syscall tests use a real Timer armed between readiness and deadline lookup, cover unbounded and future caller deadlines, and retain expired-caller controls with explicit handle/membership cleanup. No public API or scheduler behavior was changed. Current source manifest is 018b85f27e6591e5abdf7372f04567f38ce0b606152676e7b8f7cefc07d3377d (runtime-sources-after-wait-fix.json); b741adf2… was preserved byte-exact in runtime-sources-after-dma-charge-fix-v2.json, and the original tests-only baseline4c0597… remains preserved. New first failure: `ordinary_le_without_dhkey -> reuse_tag('peer-rpa', rotate=True) -> type_tag -> settled(timeout40)` could not find an encrypted notification-enabled connection. The peer's rotation returned a valid fresh RPA53:9e:44:72:2e:4d resolving to its stored identityf0:f1:f2:f3:f4:c3. Raw events show87 reconnections followed immediately by reason5 authentication disconnects. RootCanal's guest-controller14 log explicitly shows each rotated RPA resolved to the expected identity, then an actual guest Disconnect command about9ms later without an intervening encryption command. Thus the initial status summary showing no connected peer is not evidence that accept-list resolution failed; actual resolution and repeated connection occurred.

Read-only causal diagnosis: production LE_EVENT_MASK leaves Enhanced Connection Complete bit9 masked, and hci_codec parses legacy Connection Complete subevent0x01. The pinned local RootCanal implementation correctly places the raw advertising address in that legacy event; only the enhanced event substitutes resolved identity. BluetoothService.on_connected constructs the bond lookup peer directly from event address/type, unlike its advertising path, which invokes existing controller.le.resolve. A rotated RPA therefore misses the stored identity bond and reaches the explicit REASON_AUTHENTICATION disconnect path. This is a concrete production identity-lookup omission, not a controller capability exception or another HID readiness timeout. The failed run does not retain binary payloads of every HCI event; the diagnosis combines exact production mask/parser, pinned model source, controller resolution log, guest Disconnect command and peer reason5. Minimal existing-resolver correction/test is being reviewed read-only; sources remain frozen while root's modem producer runs, and no retry or runtime edit has occurred.

IMPLEMENTER'S INITIAL IMPLEMENTATION ON P02M0194 (2026-10-10 09:28:39 UTC):

Following the preserved independent peer-RPA failure, prepared a minimal runtime repair and persistent regression under ignored bluetooth-rpa-forget-preparation/. Production sources remain frozen17fec87c while the modem/admin producer owns the shared host. Nothing in this section claims application, compilation or passing tests. The reviewed complete staged patch is3651d14b7d42876995611030b1608b3bda863706d12b5270b9f86cc6f4a991a0; runtime-only patch242d7236138778d96d622ffc0107292120a7f8d044cfeb89ebbb100f803e10a9. The previously reviewed fcee318e bundle and initial combined939e5652 patch are preserved.

Stack::on_connected resolves a successful raw-random legacy connection address through the existing cached-IRK resolver before duplicate admission, pairing-attempt identity and bond lookup. Failed completion handling and non-random/controller-resolved identity types retain their behavior. The original connection peer is retained separately only for the sole private start_le_pairing call: SMP c1/f5/f6 still receive actual connection address/type, while Link.peer, legacy eligibility and operator progress use the canonical identity. No Link field, public API or enhanced-event support is added.

A related operator failure was established by read-only code tracing and accepted by root: after durable forget, stale local le.irks can canonicalize a fresh RPA scan to the deleted identity, while reconnect_bonded rebuilds the controller resolving list from remaining eligible stored bonds and no longer maps that identity. pair_le then addresses an identity that cannot match the still-private advertiser. When no eligible bond remains, reconnect_bonded returns before clearing the controller list; no universal failure is claimed for that distinct branch. The staged forget change removes and scrubs only matching cached IRKs immediately after successful durable deletion. Failed deletion preserves live state and key; no unrelated cache/controller refactor is included.

Prepared src/tools/bluetooth_le_reconnect.py and bluetooth-le-reconnect.rs as a persistent proof called from the existing registered independent checker's unconditional self_test. It extracts actual production resolver, on_connected, start_le_pairing, forget, scan mapping, pair, pair_le and set_aside plus real AES/CMAC/key/HCI codecs; durable-storage/controller/SMP-start boundaries are mocked. Eighteen cases cover raw versus identity addresses, SC/legacy key selection, refusal and duplicate cases, raw SMP peer preservation, successful forget/fresh scan/raw-address re-pair command, and failed deletion retaining its key. Five causal mutants are prepared. The original reconnect and original forget negatives, staged positive proof and mutants remain UNPERFORMED. The --forget-only diagnostic is explicitly restricted to expected-failure mode so it cannot silently skip positive cases or overstate a count. Root accepted runtime/test scope; second review is pending before the exclusive slot is released.

The prepared ignored evidence runner run-staged-proof.py7fa22d9e… uses the unchanged reviewed admission/owned-process helper, CPU0–3/nice10/jobs2, original source pins and fresh outputs; it stops the first unexpected result. Planned canonical restaging is owned by the USB agent on all three architectures, followed by the already prepared DEFAULT-only image runner3954b4c9… for a new real kernel/bootable volume/ISO. The affected bluetooth-le fixture and independent gate must run on that new image after authorization; unrelated earlier fixture proofs retain their actual historical provenance. Concrete read-only diagnosis: btclassic retains initial tag raw-RPA scan result and intentionally calls pair on it for the fresh SC downgrade attempt after bonding. pair_le still stores that raw peer in Attempt, while new on_connected resolves its connection event to canonical identity; pairing_peer therefore becomes false and the link is refused as unrequested before SMP. The18-case host proof covered unknown raw attempts and known canonical attempts, but missed an active known raw attempt from a retained scan result. Root and an independent reviewer confirmed the path. A minimal follow-up is being staged outside production: retain original scan address for command authorization/connection/SMP, normalize bond policy/link lookup/legacy/Attempt bookkeeping within pair_le, and resolve a raw HCI peer before failure matching too so failed completion terminates a canonical attempt. Regression must extract actual Controller.fail_attempt and exercise actual pair→successful/failed completion, legacy-over-SC refusal, public/static/resolved identity types and unrelated failures. No guest assertion or security criterion is changed; production6acea remains frozen while this staged correction is reviewed.

### ACTUAL MSBC DUPLEX PASSED; WHOLE RUN FAILED ON CVSD PLAYBACK (2026-10-10 18:34:32 UTC)

Actual current-source 0b10 whole independent run, unified session 4809, **FAILED after 534.658 s**, exit 1, using the strict derived image receipt 8eced183 and unchanged canonical guest image b23b1d92. The fresh output is `bluetooth-after-wideband-bridge-fix/gates/independent`; its exact original invocation and command log remain retained. All original deadlines, independent signal thresholds, microphone assertions and existing exact controller exceptions were preserved. There are 43 completed scenario rows, including both operator lifecycle scenarios and the real late-SCO completion refusal. This is not a canonical/composite whole-gate PASS.

The actual `classic-voice-msbc` scenario **PASSED**, including incoming/answer/active/hangup, independent playback decode (29,520 samples; RMS 3882.364; dominant 333 Hz), separate microphone/speaker controls and retained microphone gain across voice-owner close/reopen. Its actual guest microphone mean-square was 12,411,032 versus the independently decoded post-gain reference 12,480,864.038, ratio 0.9944049. The wideband alternate 6/63-byte setting opened and closed through the actual driver, bridge and controller. These results establish mSBC acceptance in this run; they do not establish the remaining CVSD/HSP modes.

CVSD did open alternate 2/17-byte USB voice, establish the link and answer the application call. It then failed the unchanged independent playback assertion: 15,180 captured samples, final-half-second RMS 0.0, with a nonzero independent microphone reference (mean-square 12,499,506.5 at 8 kHz). Retained `radio/headset-voice.pcm` contains only 780 nonzero samples, all at the expected +/-4000 tone amplitude, and ends with 4,140 zero samples. The peer continued exchanging real 120-byte SCO payloads; the pinned RootCanal path forwards their bytes unchanged. CVSD duplex/microphone completion, HSP and the later transfer/privacy/restart/cold phases were **NOT PASSED/UNPERFORMED**, as applicable.

Read-only diagnosis identified a concrete scheduling weakness: `Stack::drain_packets` drains a controller's receive stream without a bound; every SCO reply synchronously waits for the final USB ISO piece. A CVSD packet plus its HCI header needs eight 17-byte full-speed service intervals, versus one 63-byte interval for mSBC. Controller receive channels also precede PCM and ordinary client channels in the waitset, while the kernel selects the first ready channel. Continuous receive traffic can therefore prevent PCM replenishment and call/control handling. Actual CVSD traffic averaged about 8.56 ms inward and 8.61 ms outward with intermittent brief tone bursts, consistent with this mechanism. The logs do not trace individual PCM IPC scheduling, so this is a source-grounded causal hypothesis, not a proved exclusive explanation of the live failure. An exact-source scheduling regression is being prepared before a runtime correction is considered; no assertion, peer timing or capability exception has been weakened.

Actual clean-release `slot-release-after-failure.json` SHA-256 `eaba7e6ff1f5c61b6ace3eb43e00f702a5084c33b7ae2a2ec61d527965cd5613` verifies all 57 observed owned identities including coordinator PID3528244/start8786706 absent, all 21 controls absent/no QEMU, unchanged 0b10/full 2,854 source paths, all 9 image/22 original input/15 protected/24 media identities unchanged, 8 private seed copies removed and private TMP absent. No retry was launched. P02M0194 remains OPEN and earlier audit content is retained.

Current sourcec23a53 includes the separately reviewed host-only brightness transport; the relevant Bluetooth and audioprobe bytes remain unchanged from source068ac4. P02M0194 remains OPEN.

Read-only diagnosis now identifies the cause. PermissionManager's main receive buffer is only 512 bytes, although its typed pipeline client encodes the complete arguments, stages and environment. The kernel's capability-aware receive correctly refuses an oversized frame without consuming it (`receive_transactionally`); the existing runtime maps that receive error to `Closed`, so the manager's root serve loop ends. Its exit closes the unique display/input/audio minting roots. Those services explicitly exit when their admin peer closes, then virtio-gpu/virtio-snd/HDA exit when their provider client closes. Supervisor's generic peer-close report says `crashed`; the retained log shows no initiating kernel fault, memory-pressure event or missed heartbeat (the driver incidents record a fresh Pong, opcode 8). No host-crash claim follows from this guest failure.

Beginning a private, reviewable candidate for the minimal required integration repair: size this service's heap-backed receive storage to the existing `rt::MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` transport ceiling, retaining typed validation and every existing oracle. The existing real-broker regression will be extended to create the actual payload through echo/redirection, read back its exact bytes and demonstrate subsequent broker/service liveness. These are preparation goals, not completed behavior or passed verification. No canonical runtime file has changed, no import/compiler/test/QEMU command has run for this candidate, and no milestone completion status changes.

## IMPLEMENTER'S INITIAL IMPLEMENTATION ON P02M0194 (2026-10-12 03:07:57 UTC):

Session goal: finish every open milestone except P02M0105/P02M0106, or record what cannot be finished. This entry records the PAN DHCP failure's cause and fix, a harness fix that made the serial burst deterministic, and the furthest independent run so far. The milestone stays OPEN at this entry (the whole gate has not passed yet).

1. PAN DHCP - ROOT CAUSE ESTABLISHED, FIXED IN THE GUEST. dnsmasq pings an address before offering it and stays deaf to DHCP for its `PING_WAIT` of 3 s (dnsmasq source: `config.h` PING_WAIT 3, `dnsmasq.c` icmp_ping -> delay_dhcp). NetworkService's client (`do_dhcp` in `src/user/services/core/src/network_service.rs`) sent ONE DISCOVER and gave up after 2 s (DHCP_TIMEOUT_TICKS 200), so it fell back to the static configuration before every dnsmasq OFFER arrived; RFC 2131 4.1 puts a client's first retransmission at 4 s for that reason. The same single-shot client also explains the "DHCP unanswered" lines in slow aarch64 boots with slirp (a lost first DISCOVER was never sent again). FIX: DISCOVER sent up to 3 times and REQUEST up to 2 times, 2 s apart (DHCP_RETRANSMIT_TICKS/DHCP_DISCOVERS/DHCP_REQUESTS), rebuilt each time because ARP answers share the buffer, same transaction id; a closed link ends the wait. Diagnostics: `bluetooth_transfer_peers.py` now emits a `pan-frame` event per PAN frame (direction, BNEP type, destination, ethertype, IPv4 UDP ports and the DHCP message type), test-only.
   OBSERVED IN THE NEXT RUN (`.build/logs/bluetooth-independent/20261012-024351`): DISCOVER at t=0, retransmitted at +2.0 s, dnsmasq's OFFER at +3.0 s, REQUEST, ACK; lease 10.94.0.20 committed in dnsmasq.leases; guest "network: configured via DHCP - 10.94.0.20/24 via 10.94.0.1"; scenario `classic-pan` PASS (ping/TCP/withdrawal included).
2. SERIAL BURST MADE DETERMINISTIC (harness). `btserial` requires its 64000-byte burst to meet backpressure (`again > 0`). Bumble's DLC returns a credit for every received frame whatever its application does, so the harness's echo device never stopped granting credits and backpressure appeared only when the radio path happened to be slower than the writer: one run had 45 `Again`, run 20261012-022922 had 0 and FAILED with the data intact. `SerialPeer` in `src/harness/bluetooth_classic_peers.py` now models a device with a bounded echo buffer (stops granting credits past 4096 unsent echo bytes, resumes at 1024), which is what btserial's own comment assumes ("the device's credits and its echo backlog"); Bumble's framing and credit accounting are unchanged. Next run: "53 answered again", btserial PASS, peer received >= 64000 bytes.
3. RUNS (x86_64, development image built by the gate, SMP2): run 20261012-022101 did not start (the development build refused stale audit-linked abiprobe/vkprobe after the rt::pool change; `src/tools/foreign-audit-link.py --check` PASS 79 s re-linked them). Run 20261012-022922 FAIL at btserial (above). Run 20261012-024351 (1132 s): PASS through LE pairing models, classic pairing/downgrade/policy, HID input, classic-serial, music/route/AVRCP/disconnect, late voice, mSBC/CVSD/HSP voice, OPP and its consent bound, classic-pan, broadcast-to-A2DP clear and encrypted, host-RPA reuse, periodic privacy, domain-after-load, service stop/start - then FAIL at `reuse_tag(g, 'restart')`: after BluetoothService was restarted the bonded LE HID peer ("remote", advertising a resolvable private address) was not reconnected within the 40 s settle window. Investigation is in progress with an HCI trace (`BT_BRIDGE_HCI_TRACE`, opcodes/event codes/status only, added to `usbredir_device.py`).


## IMPLEMENTER'S INITIAL IMPLEMENTATION ON P02M0194 (2026-10-12 05:47:54 UTC):

Continuation of the entry above: the restart failure's cause found and fixed, and the milestone's last two items closed on actual runs.

4. A RESTARTED BLUETOOTHSERVICE RECONNECTED NO BONDED LE DEVICE USING A PRIVATE ADDRESS - ROOT CAUSE AND FIX. The HCI trace of the restart (run 20261012-030717) showed the accept-list add (0x2011) but no resolving-list add (0x2027) and no LE Set Random Address (0x2005): the new process had read neither its own IRK nor the peer's. The bond store's capability (role BONDS, kind "client") is a duplicate of the store's shared root; `ChannelTransport::call` takes "the next message" as the reply, and both the raw `bond()`/`store_bond()` exchanges and the generated client used correlation 1, so a reply the store sent to the killed predecessor shifted every later reply by one - list-after-list still parsed, keyed lookups did not. FIX (`src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service.rs`): `BondTransport` stamps every bond-store request with a correlation this process has not used (seeded randomly once), returns only the reply carrying it (caller's correlation restored), and scrubs and drops anything else; used by `bond()`, `store_bond()` and the generated client. Next run (20261012-033004): the restart issues 0x2005, 0x2011, 0x2027, 0x2043 and `ordinary-le-reuse-restart` and `keyboard-restart` PASS.
5. RESULTS ON ONE TREE (x86_64): `./check.sh --gate bluetooth-independent` PASS 1510 s (80 PASS rows; "PASS supported independent scenarios, production USB transport and bond persistence; 5 explicit per-feature fixture requirements remain in results.json" - LE SC-dependent features RootCanal's controller cannot do, held by the in-guest fixture gates as the oracle item provides). Fixture gates, BOOT_IMAGE = the same development image: bluetooth-codecs PASS 33 s, bluetooth-service PASS 841 s, bluetooth-input PASS 240 s, bluetooth-le PASS 331 s, bluetooth-audio PASS 360 s, bluetooth-transfer PASS 400 s, bluetooth-le-audio PASS 381 s. bluetooth-classic FAILED twice on a race in its probe (`btclassic` closed its pairing watcher and immediately asked the controller's state / started a pairing, which the service could serve before it saw the close: first "the radio was ... still pairable", then the host declared DisplayYesNo and Passkey Entry instead of refusing Just Works). FIX (`src/user/services/core/src/btclassic.rs`): `unwatch()` waits up to 2 s for the service's own `pairable=false` (`pairable` is exactly `watcher != 0`); assertions unchanged. Rerun on a freshly built development image: bluetooth-classic PASS 970 s (pair, policy, serial, reuse, reuse after cold boot, forget).
6. Harness/tooling changes, test-only: `SerialPeer` bounded echo buffer; `pan-frame` trace events; `BT_BRIDGE_HCI_TRACE` (opcodes/event codes/status only, never parameters) in `usbredir_device.py`.
7. Items ticked in docs/todo/P02M0194.md: `btctl` and THE ORACLE; no unchecked item remains, and the TODO.md row is marked COMPLETE (2026-10-12). `./check.sh --gate milestone-index` PASS.

Drivers host suite: 578 PASS. The fixture's 7.5 ms path stays, covered by `random_frames_round_trip` and `arbitrary_bytes_are_read_or_refused`.

AUDITOR'S REVIEW ON P02M0194 (2026-10-15T04:36:14Z):

**Rating: 5/10.** The implementation is large and mostly careful. The protocol leaves are pure, host-tested and bounds-checked: L2CAP and ERTM, RFCOMM with its FCS and credits, SDP with its continuation, OBEX, AVDTP, the HFP AT parser, ISO reassembly and the BASE parser. The SMP initiator is correct: it commits before it reveals, checks the confirm value and the DHKey check, refuses a reflected public key and insists on 16-octet keys. The two-axis no-downgrade rule is applied on both radios and to cross-transport keys. The inbound policy is enforced by PSM and by RFCOMM server channel. The bond store commits durably and rolls back on a failed write. The restart correlation fix in `BondTransport` is sound. `btctl` covers every listed command, THREAT_MODEL 2.4 has the required content, and the gates are registered in `check.sh` and `release-required.toml`. The LC3 Appendix C removal is consistent: nothing in the tree still refers to the deleted files, and conformance is held by `bluetooth-codecs`. It is not counted against the milestone. However, two High defects break core promises on paths no gate takes. First, the bond store's `list` answers from a 1 KiB buffer. Past a modest number of bonds (7-21, against the stated 64), the stack sees no bonds at all, and with them it loses trust, reconnection, IRKs and every no-downgrade check. Second, the standing LE accept-list attempt is neither refreshed nor re-armed. Of several absent bonded LE peripherals only one can come back, and a coordinated set returns as a single earbud. Two Medium defects follow. The host's own private address does not rotate while that attempt is out, which is the steady state whenever a bonded LE peer is absent. BR/EDR links are accepted as encrypted without checking the negotiated key size (KNOB). Smaller deviations make up the rest: the OPP SDP record is always offered, the OBEX packet bound is not the stated one, the grant and subscription bounds are loose, and LE `pair-legacy` withholds Secure Connections.

## Findings

1. **The bond store answers `list` from a 1 KiB reply buffer, so past about 7-21 bonds every policy lookup sees no bond at all (High, Defect).**
   - **The buffer.** `BluetoothBondStore` serves with `let mut reply: [u8; 1024]` (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_bond_store.rs:372-374).
   - **Record size.** A keyless `bond-record` on the wire is 44 bytes, plus its name (up to 48), its alias (up to 32 through `btctl alias`) and one byte per trust bit (generated `BondRecord::write`, /data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/libs/protocol/bluetooth-proto/src/generated/liber/bluetooth/v1.rs:7247-7293). The list header adds 7 bytes.
   - **The host's own record is always listed.** The store's "this host" identity record (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/le.rs:111) always occupies part of the reply.
   - **Capacity against the bound.**
     - 21 bonds fit with empty names, 15 with 16-byte names and 7 with full names and aliases.
     - A dual-mode device bonded through CTKD takes two records.
     - The milestone states 64 records ("bonds 64 records in 64 kB"; /data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/bond_store.rs:28 `MAX_RECORDS = 64`). 64 full records need 9,351 bytes.
   - **What the stack does with the overflow.** The generated dispatch replaces an outgrown reply with `Err(Again)` (v1.rs:7528-7568). `Stack::records` turns any error into an empty list (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service.rs:936-945), and `record()` (948-951) inherits that empty list.
   - **Downgrade protection is lost.** Every decision then runs as if nothing were bonded, so the no-downgrade refusals are skipped:
     - a lower-level link key (bluetooth_service/classic.rs:807-814);
     - Just Works over an authenticated bond (classic.rs:762-771);
     - a PIN over a better bond (classic.rs:785-789);
     - LE re-pairing (le.rs:230-236, 370-378);
     - LE legacy over a Secure Connections bond (bluetooth_service.rs:2002-2004).
   - **Re-pairing resets trust.** A re-pair is stored with trust, enabled and alias reset (le.rs:381, classic.rs:817).
   - **Bonded peers are refused or cannot reconnect.**
     - Bonded trusted peers' pages are rejected (classic.rs:584), and page scan goes off (classic.rs:1008).
     - LE reconnection and the IRK table are empty (le.rs:169, 132-145).
     - Grants find no alias (bluetooth_service/gatt.rs:212, bluetooth_service/serial.rs:122).
     - `btctl list` and `bonded` fail (bluetooth_service.rs:1801-1809).
   - **Fix.** No test lists more than a handful of bonds. Size the store's reply buffer for 64 full records, as the PermissionManager receive buffer was sized to the transport ceiling.

2. **The standing LE accept-list attempt is neither refreshed nor re-armed, so only one of several absent bonded peripherals can come back (High, Defect).**
   `reconnect_bonded` returns at once while `le.accepting` is set (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/le.rs:165). Three paths are affected:
   - (a) **A second disconnect is not listed.** `on_disconnected` calls only `reconnect_bonded` (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service.rs:1331-1332). A second bonded LE peer that leaves while the attempt for the first is out is never put on the filter accept list.
   - (b) **No re-arm after a reconnection.** LE Create Connection completes with the first device it connects. `on_connected` clears `accepting` (1208), and its reconnect branch (1250-1273) never arms a new attempt for the peers still absent. A new attempt is armed only on:
     - an LE disconnect;
     - a trust change or forget (`relist`, 1839 and 2068);
     - a new bond (le.rs:426);
     - a GATT mint (gatt.rs:231);
     - controller readiness (le.rs:129).
   - (c) **No re-arm after a failed operator pairing.** An operator LE pairing sets the attempt aside (le.rs:211-217, 243). If that connection never completes, the 60-second cancel arrives in `on_connected` with a non-zero status and `relist` false (1217-1225). The standing attempt is then never taken up again, contrary to le.rs:10-12.

   Effects:
   - **Two LE input devices.** After a reboot or a BluetoothService restart with an LE keyboard and an LE mouse, whichever advertises first reconnects. The other stays out for as long as the first is connected.
   - **LE Audio sets.** A coordinated set comes back as one earbud. The set search deliberately skips members that are already bonded (bluetooth_service/le_audio.rs:388) and relies on the accept list. When the 10-second search ends, the device is offered with the one member it has (le_audio.rs:291-303).

   This contradicts parts b (several LE links, "bonded peripherals reconnect through the controller's filter accept list"), c (reconnection by the device after a restart) and h (earbuds driven as one device). The gates reconnect the tag and the remote strictly one after the other (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/btclassic.rs:1088-1106), which this logic happens to satisfy.

   Fix: relist (cancel and re-arm) when a bonded LE peer leaves while an attempt is out. Re-arm after an accept-list connection completes, and after a cancelled operator attempt.

3. **The host's own resolvable private address does not rotate while the standing attempt is out, and a refused rotation goes unnoticed (Medium, Defect).**
   - **The condition.** The 15-minute rotation runs only when `!controller.le.accepting` (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service.rs:1645-1650). Nothing cancels the attempt in order to rotate.
   - **How long it lasts.** The attempt stays out for as long as any bonded LE peer trusted for input, GATT or audio is absent (bluetooth_service/le.rs:163-194). A machine whose LE mouse is switched off therefore keeps one private address indefinitely. Part b requires the address "rotated every fifteen minutes" (docs/todo/P02M0194.md:232).
   - **Why the gate misses it.** The independent gate's `privacy_rotation` keeps its only LE peer connected through the 905-second wait (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/harness/bluetooth_guest_le.py:293-305), so the attempt is never out.
   - **A refused rotation is recorded anyway.** The rotation condition does not exclude a running scan (operator, broadcast or set search). The HCI specification has the controller refuse LE Set Random Address while scanning or initiating is enabled. `rotate_address` records the new address as soon as the command is queued:
     - `Controller::command` returns true on queueing (bluetooth_service.rs:554-567; le.rs:155-157);
     - nothing handles that opcode's status.
   - **Consequence of a refused rotation.** New links take `link.local` from `le.own` (bluetooth_service.rs:1243), and SMP authenticates with that address (le.rs:306). LE pairing then fails its confirm or DHKey check until a later rotation succeeds.
   - **Fix.** Rotate through the `relist` cancel and re-arm path, and set `le.own` only when the command succeeds.

4. **BR/EDR links are treated as encrypted whatever key length the controllers negotiated (Medium, Defect).**
   - **What the host checks.** `classic_encryption` marks the link encrypted on any successful Encryption Change (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/classic.rs:619-632). The held profile channels are then released on it (classic.rs:666-671).
   - **What it never asks.** The host never issues HCI Read Encryption Key Size. The command is absent from /data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/hci_bredr.rs:17-59 and from the service.
   - **The attack.** LMP's key-size negotiation is unauthenticated, so an attacker on the radio can force a 1-octet encryption key (KNOB, CVE-2019-9506). This works under a bond that `btctl` reports as "Secure Connections, authenticated". LE is not affected because SMP insists on 16 octets (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/smp_pairing.rs:490).
   - **Why it matters here.** "Encryption required on every link that carries user data" and the two-axis levels both assume the encryption has the key's strength.
   - **Fix.** After Encryption Change on BR/EDR, read the key size before releasing held channels. Disconnect below 16 octets for a Secure Connections key and below 7 otherwise. The in-guest fixture controller can report a short key as the oracle.

5. **The Object Push SDP record is always offered, not only while a `btctl receive` waits (Low, Defect).**
   - **What the code does.** `ControllerState::new` offers `sdp::opp_server(0x0001_0007, OPP_CHANNEL, None)` alongside the always-on roles (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/classic.rs:112). Nothing ever withdraws it: `sdp::Server::withdraw` (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/sdp.rs:740) has no caller, and `receiver_started` and `end_receiver` (bluetooth_service/opp.rs:365, 461) do not touch the server.
   - **What the milestone says.** Part a asks for the OPP record "while a `btctl receive` waits". Its DONE note claims "the OPP server on channel 9 offered only while a `btctl receive` waits" (docs/todo/P02M0194.md:187-198), which does not hold.
   - **Impact.** The RFCOMM admission still refuses the channel (classic.rs:1603-1614), so the effect is a service advertised to any connected peer while it is not running.
   - **Gate coverage.** No gate checks for the record's absence; btclassic.rs:604-608 checks only the voice gateway and HID.
   - **Fix.** Offer the record in `receiver_started` and withdraw it when the receiver ends.

6. **OBEX offers a 4096-byte maximum packet instead of the stated bound, and `bt_bounds::OBEX_PACKET` is never used (Low, Defect).**
   - **The stated bound.** Part a states "OBEX's maximum packet is offered as the same 1691" (docs/todo/P02M0194.md:87-89), and /data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/bt_bounds.rs:24 defines `OBEX_PACKET = BREDR_MTU`.
   - **What the code uses instead.** Nothing reads `OBEX_PACKET`.
     - The push client's CONNECT and the server's CONNECT answer both state `MAX_PACKET = 4096` (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/obex.rs:24, 297, 481).
     - The client clamps the peer's maximum to 4096 (obex.rs:385).
     - Both assemblers are sized by it (bluetooth_service/opp.rs:174, 366).
   - **Interop consequence.** Over GOEP's L2CAP transport each OBEX packet is one SDU. `ertm_send` (bluetooth_service/classic.rs:1725-1732) does not limit it to the channel's `peer_mtu`, so a push can send an SDU larger than the peer configured whenever the peer's OBEX maximum exceeds its MTU.
   - **Fix.** Offer `OBEX_PACKET`, and clamp pushed packets to the smaller of the peer's OBEX maximum and the channel's peer MTU.

7. **Application grants are capped at twice the stated figure, and a GATT grant's subscriptions are not capped at all (Low, Defect).**
   - **Grant count.** Part a states "32 minted grants". GATT grants and serial grants are each capped at `MINTED_GRANTS` separately (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/gatt.rs:25, serial.rs:17), which allows 64 together.
   - **Subscriptions are unbounded.**
     - Within one grant, each successful `subscribe` adds another producer handle, with no limit and no check for an existing subscription to the same value (gatt.rs:497).
     - Closed consumers are pruned only when that attribute next notifies (gatt.rs:564-585).
   - **Consequence.** A single application holding a GATT grant can grow BluetoothService's handle table toward the 256-handle Domain (`bt_bounds::DOMAIN_HANDLES`). Channel creation then fails for prompts, pushes, PCM channels and new clients, which breaks part a's assumption that every table is bounded inside the derived Domain.
   - **Fix.** Use one bound for both grant kinds, and cap or de-duplicate subscriptions per grant.

8. **`btctl pair-legacy` on LE never offers Secure Connections, so a peer that supports it is bonded at the LE legacy level (Low, Optional).**
   - **What the code does.** With `legacy` set, `start_le_pairing` passes no public key (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/le.rs:306). The pairing request then carries no Secure Connections bit (/data/yellow/libersystem/src/user/services/logic/src/smp_pairing.rs:289), so the exchange is legacy whatever the peer supports. `pair_le` checks only that no Secure Connections bond exists (le.rs:232-236).
   - **What the milestone allows.** Part b allows LE legacy only "for a peer that has nothing better" (docs/todo/P02M0194.md:236-237). BR/EDR's `pair-legacy` meets that by protocol, because a PIN is requested only from a peer without Simple Pairing.
   - **Fix.** Pass the controller's public key when it has one, and let `options.legacy` permit the legacy fall-back only when the peer's response lacks Secure Connections. The operator's explicit legacy path is kept. RootCanal's runs are unchanged, because its controller reports no P-256 support and so has no public key to offer.

## Verification performed

- **Method.** Static, read-only review against docs/todo/P02M0194.md (requirements, DONE notes and the 2026-10-14 LC3 removal) and the tail of this file. No build, test, cargo, QEMU or gate was run. Git state was not touched, and only this file was written.
- **Security and pairing paths read in full.**
  - bluetooth_service.rs: bond store client and `BondTransport`, event dispatch, `on_connected`, `on_encryption`, timers, operator verbs, `pair`, `set_trust`, `devices`.
  - bluetooth_service/classic.rs: pairing prompts, link keys, inbound policy, L2CAP signalling, CTKD, RFCOMM dispatch.
  - bluetooth_service/le.rs.
  - service_logic `bt_pairing`, `bt_policy`, `smp_pairing`, `rfcomm`, `l2cap`, `l2cap_bredr` (signalling and ERTM), `sdp` server, `obex`, `avdtp`, `hfp`, `gtbs`, `uplink`, `bt_bounds`.
  - bluetooth_bond_store.rs and `bond_store` bounds.
- **Audio and transfer paths read.**
  - bluetooth_service/{voice, broadcast, opp, serial, gatt}.rs.
  - Parts of bluetooth_service/{le_audio, audio, a2dp}.rs.
  - `le_iso` reassembly and classification.
  - drivers' `bt_usb` reassembly.
  - InputService's Bluetooth slot.
  - NetworkService's PAN links.
  - AudioService's route endpoints.
- **Bond list capacity (finding 1).** Computed from the generated `BondRecord::write` and enum widths, and confirmed against the generated dispatch's overflow fallback (v1.rs:7528-7568). I found no caller that falls back from `list` to per-peer lookups.
- **Reconnection and rotation (findings 2 and 3).** Traced every caller of `reconnect_bonded` and `relist`, and every write of `le.accepting`. Checked the gates' reconnection sequence (btclassic.rs:1088-1106) and the harness rotation scenario (bluetooth_guest_le.py:293-305).
- **Key size (finding 4).** Searched for any HCI Read Encryption Key Size or key-size handling in hci_bredr.rs, hci_codec.rs and the service; there is none.
- **OPP record (finding 5).** Searched for every caller of `sdp::Server::offer` and `withdraw`.
- **Gates, docs and tools.** Checked gate registration (check.sh:75-83; src/tools/verify-model/model/release-required.toml:69-77), THREAT_MODEL 2.4, the subcommand dispatch and helpers of btctl.rs, and the bluetooth.lsidl contracts.
- **LC3 removal.** Searched for references to the removed Appendix C data; none remain (only historical comments in lc3.rs, lc3/tests.rs and bt_lc3.rs).
- **Reviewed with no finding.** The SMP state machine, the two-axis level rules, the prompt deadline and watcher handling, the HFP gateway and mSBC H2 framing, PushServer bounds, the broadcast-code scrubbing, the PAN uplink rule and the Domain derivation in service_manager/bootstrap.rs:279.

IMPLEMENTER'S RESPONSE ON P02M0194 (2026-10-17T01:44:22Z):

All eight findings were verified against the tree and accepted.

**Finding 1 - the bond store answers `list` from a 1 KiB reply (High): ACCEPTED.**
- `src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_bond_store.rs` serves from a 10 KiB reply buffer, sized for 64 records at the largest listed size.
- New host test `src/user/libs/protocol/bluetooth-proto/src/tests.rs::sixty_four_listed_bonds_at_their_wire_bounds_are_one_reply_of_under_ten_kibibytes`:
  - 148 bytes a record, so 7 + 64 x 148 = 9479 bytes;
  - it fails at 1024 bytes, which hold 6 records.
- The same defect as P02M0180 finding 2.

**Finding 2 - the standing accept-list attempt is neither refreshed nor re-armed (High): ACCEPTED.**
All in `src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service.rs`:
- `on_disconnected` (LE) calls `relist`: cancel the attempt that is out, then re-arm it with the new list once the cancellation lands.
- `on_connected`, on success through the list: calls `reconnect_bonded` at once for the bonded peers still absent.
- `on_connected`, on a non-zero status:
  - a cancellation for a relist re-arms;
  - an operator attempt that ended without a link - refused, or cancelled at 60 s - takes the standing attempt up again;
  - the standing attempt failing on its own is not re-armed there, which would retry a controller's refusal in a loop.

The oracle: `btclassic le` (`btclassic.rs`) now disconnects the tag and the remote at once and requires both back through the accept list, each encrypted. Before the fix the remote stayed out while the tag was connected.

**Finding 3 - the private address does not rotate while the attempt is out, and a refused rotation goes unnoticed (Medium): ACCEPTED.**
In `src/user/services/core/src/bluetooth_service/le.rs`:
- `LeState` gains `rotate` and `pending_own`.
- `rotate_address` only asks for the address; it becomes `own` in `address_set`, which runs on the `LE_SET_RANDOM_ADDRESS` completion (dispatched from `bluetooth_service.rs`):
  - accepted, it becomes `own`;
  - refused, the old address stays and the rotation is asked again after `ROTATE_RETRY_TICKS` (10 s).
- `rotate_due` rotates at once when no attempt is out. When one is, it cancels it with `rotate` and `relist` set; the address is set when the cancellation lands, and the attempt is re-armed from it.
- `run_timers` no longer skips the rotation while the standing attempt is out, only while the operator's own attempt is connecting.
- `end_session` clears all three marks.

No guest oracle: the rotation is 15 minutes, which a gate cannot spend, and the fixture does not refuse LE Set Random Address. The milestone says so.

**Finding 4 - BR/EDR links treated as encrypted whatever the negotiated key size (KNOB) (Medium): ACCEPTED.**
- `src/user/services/logic/src/hci_bredr.rs` gains:
  - `READ_ENCRYPTION_KEY_SIZE` (0x1408), with `UNKNOWN_COMMAND`;
  - the reply decoder `encryption_key_size`;
  - `key_size_holds(octets, secure_connections)`: 16 octets for a Secure Connections link key, 7 otherwise.
- Host test `hci_bredr/tests.rs::a_bredr_key_shorter_than_its_link_key_promises_does_not_hold`.
- `bluetooth_service/classic.rs`:
  - after a successful Encryption Change, `classic_encryption` asks the key size;
  - `classic_key_size` disconnects below the bound;
  - only `encryption_confirmed` marks the link encrypted and releases held channels;
  - a controller answering `UNKNOWN_COMMAND` is accepted as before.
- The oracle:
  - `src/idl/bluetooth.lsidl` gains `fixture-action.key-size = 32`;
  - `bt_world.rs` answers 0x1408 with the size its device negotiated;
  - `btclassic knob` requires a 1-octet link dropped before its channel opens and a 16-octet one kept;
  - `check-bluetooth-classic.sh` requires the verdict and the service's reason line.

**Finding 5 - the Object Push SDP record is always offered (Low): ACCEPTED.**
- `bluetooth_service/classic.rs`: `ControllerState::new` no longer offers it; `OPP_RECORD` names its handle.
- `bluetooth_service/opp.rs`: `receiver_started` offers the record and `end_receiver` withdraws it. With the record gone, the RFCOMM admission refuses channel 9 too.
- The oracle:
  - The fixture's phone (`bt_world/opp.rs`, `opp_searched`) searches the host's records for 0x1105 before it pushes, says how many it found, and pushes anyway.
  - `btclassic transfer` requires `found 0 records` with nobody waiting.
  - `check-bluetooth-transfer.sh` requires `found 1 records` while `btctl receive` waits.
  - The OPP disconnect self-test (`src/tools/bluetooth-opp-disconnect.rs`, `bluetooth_opp_disconnect.py`) models the record. It was watched failing with the offer removed (`OPP_RECORD_NOT_OFFERED`) and with the withdraw removed (`OPP_RECORD_NOT_WITHDRAWN`).

**Finding 6 - OBEX offers 4096 instead of the stated 1691, and SDUs can exceed the peer's MTU (Low): ACCEPTED.**
- `src/user/services/logic/src/obex.rs`:
  - `MAX_PACKET = bt_bounds::OBEX_PACKET`;
  - `PushClient::limit(transport_max)` clamps every packet to the channel's MTU, never below OBEX's 255-byte minimum.
- `opp.rs::opp_l2cap_opened` applies the channel's `peer_mtu` before CONNECT.
- New host test `obex/tests.rs::the_packet_size_is_the_bound_and_the_transports`. It was watched failing both with 4096 and without the MTU clamp.

**Finding 7 - grants capped at twice the stated figure, and subscriptions unbounded (Low): ACCEPTED.**
- `bluetooth_service/gatt.rs` adds `Stack::minted()`, so GATT and serial grants share the stated 32 (`serial.rs` uses it too).
- A subscription to a value the grant already holds replaces the old one, whose stream ends.
- A grant holds at most 4 subscriptions (`GRANT_SUBSCRIPTIONS`), and all grants together at most 32 (`ALL_SUBSCRIPTIONS`); past either, the answer is `Exhausted`.
- The oracle: `btgatt again` subscribes twice to one value and requires the notification on the second stream and the first stream ended. `check-bluetooth-le.sh` runs it. It is not in plain `btgatt`, because the independent harness counts exactly one CCCD write per ordinary run.
- The per-grant cap of 4 is not exercised in a guest: the fixture offers three values.

**Finding 8 - `pair-legacy` on LE never offers Secure Connections (Low, Optional): ACCEPTED.**
- `bluetooth_service/le.rs::start_le_pairing` passes the controller's public key whenever it has one.
- `smp_pairing` already sets the Secure Connections bit when a key is present, and admits legacy only when the response lacks it and the operator asked for legacy.
- The oracle: `btclassic le` forgets the display and pairs it with `pair-legacy`. It must bond at Secure Connections, authenticated; the fixture's display refuses a request that does not offer it, so the old code fails.

Everything builds. The milestone records all eight under "Corrections of 2026-10-15".

**Found while running the gates, and fixed:**
- An LE pairing's end never cleared its prompt.
  - A passkey shown for an LE pairing is kept after the person's yes, for the peer's keypresses, and nothing cleared it when that pairing ended, as Simple Pairing Complete clears a BR/EDR one. So the next pairing that needed a prompt was answered no at once. Finding 8's new `pair-legacy` step met it.
  - `le.rs::le_pairing_over` now clears the prompt for the link's peer when SMP bonds or fails, and `on_disconnected` does the same when the LE link goes.
- A regression from finding 3: with the address taken as `own` only on the controller's acceptance, a connection queued right after the rotation at start was made from the public address. The independent gate's host saw the public address after a BluetoothService restart.
  - `LeState::own_type()` now counts a pending address as random. The controller runs the commands in order.
- `btgatt again` accepted only the fixture's initial custom value. A plain `btgatt` run in the same boot writes "hello", so it now accepts either.
- The exact-source proofs `bluetooth_le_reconnect` and `bluetooth_profile_lifecycle` splice the production methods, and their models were updated:
  - they mock `rotate_address`;
  - `reconnect_bonded` keeps the production guard against an operator attempt in progress;
  - they expect the re-arms of finding 2 and the public key under legacy;
  - they take `opp_l2cap_opened`'s MTU.
  - They pass, and their causal mutants fail.

**Verification (x86_64):**
- `bluetooth-classic` passed (1086 s), `btclassic knob` and `stall` among its runs.
- `bluetooth-transfer` passed, with "sdp phone found 1 records for 0x1105" while a receiver waits.
- `bluetooth-le` passed (531 s): `pair-legacy` at Secure Connections, authenticated; both peers back through the accept list; `btgatt again`.
- `bluetooth-independent` passed (1285 s). Its scenarios include the periodic privacy rotation and the private address after a restart.
- `bluetooth-service` (again on the final image), `bluetooth-input`, `bluetooth-audio`, `bluetooth-le-audio` (again on the final image) and `bluetooth-codecs` passed.
- `host-tests` passed.
